## waypoints

1. **W1723** (working) The gate trend's slope is the Theil-Sen estimate, the median of pairwise slopes, so one cold
   sample cannot move it
   - next: After W1718 lands: slope() becomes the Theil-Sen estimate (the median of the slopes of every pair of samples,
     indexed by gate), a test where one huge outlier leaves the slope at the true line's, and the least-squares mutant
     retargeted.
2. **W1803** (working) mikemol-commit bounds the git hook by CPU and a no-progress witness, not a 3 h wall timeout, and
   names the phase it was in
   - next: Derive the stall looks from the gate's own history instead of the first default (120 looks of 30 s): read the
     round spans and phase marks (query-traces, W1799) for the longest run of no CPU and no output a healthy gate
     showed, and set DEFAULT_LOOKS from it with the derivation written beside the number. Needs some days of round spans
     with phase marks.
3. **W1667** (ready) Give a manifest to each unmanifested card W1592 released, in rank order, returning each to ready as
   it gains one
   - next: List the ready cards with no manifest (nemik-stackable mtools), block them on this card with --update-many,
     then --update each with its exact files in rank order starting with the leaves of W1514, W1515 and W1516
4. **W1690** (ready) Find which second writer touched the staged tree's victim files during the refused mega gate
   - next: The witnesses run one after another inside a gate (.githooks/pre-commit witness() is synchronous), so two
     witnesses in one gate cannot collide. Find the second writer: the 18 queued stack processes I pkill'd while a gate
     ran (their children may have kept running over /var/tmp/mtools-staged), or two gates sharing that fixed path. Read
     the refused gate's timeline against the pkill time and list the processes then alive.
5. **W1691** (ready) Give each domain_witness its own copy of the staged tree so concurrent witnesses never read each
   other's probes
   - next: Measure first: build the per-witness tree as a git worktree (shared object store) at a fixed slot path
     witness-0..N so its bazel output base stays warm across gates, off /var/tmp (reaped at 6h idle, and ext4 so no
     reflink; cp --reflink=always only on a btrfs path); report the cold and warm start cost before wiring it.
6. **W1780** (ready) The gate's SMT knee and fixed --jobs are read from luthen's model trained on the collected
   throughput history
7. **W1823** (ready) Bazel witness tests for the three mypy domains (ratchet, mdstruct, hooks): one target each,
   declared inputs its victim files, checker and config, planted defect caught once
8. **W1824** (ready) Bazel witness tests for the three ruff domains (ratchet, mdstruct, hooks): one target each,
   declared inputs its victim files, checker and config, planted defect caught once
9. **W1825** (ready) Bazel witness tests for the ratchet gate (census sources and BASELINE) and the githooks shellcheck:
   one target per witness and victim, planted defect caught once
10. **W1513** (ready) pycodemod annotates from observed types and narrows them from use, applying a file only where the
   house mypy count falls
   - next: Umbrella over three steps. Measured by el-openglo via github-8f: monkeytype on its worst file took 965 house
     findings to 866 (-99), because traced types are the broad ones callers pass (bare ndarray, Dict[str, Union[...]])
     and the house flags need TypedDict, NDArray and Protocol forms, so there is no mechanical fixer for its
     36,933-finding debt. Do not pin an older interpreter for monkeytype, which crashes on 3.14's Union.
11. **W1697** (ready) mikemol-repin bumps a sibling git pin (mikemol-fence inside mikemol-hooks' dependencies) after
   each fence change, then hooks declares fence as a dependency
12. **W1701** (ready) The gate runs the bazel suite before the domain witnesses, since the witnesses cost the most and
   fail the least
   - next: After W1699 measures it: move the witness calls after the bazel suite in .githooks/pre-commit, with the
     fail-fast reason (cost high, failure rate low) in the comment, and check the gate's phase order in
     test_hooks_preflight.
13. **W1722** (ready) Per-phase median, spread and slope are published as registered series in Victoria with freshness
   rules, for a panel and a learned-baseline alert
   - next: After W1718: send each phase's median, spread and slope through the emitter traintel already uses
     (mikemol-otlp, vmsingle), registering the metric names and their freshness rules the way mikemol.train.considered
     is registered, so a panel and a learned-baseline alert read the series. Read the cited ruling and attach its
     warrant before building.
14. **W1724** (ready) Each gate's duration is recorded beside its executed-test count, cache-hit share and the host's
   load, so the suite's slope is split into its causes
   - next: After W1715's per-round record exists: add to it the executed-test count and the cache-hit share (from the
     invocation's BES summary, 'N tests, M cached') and the host's one-minute load at the gate's start and end, so each
     gate's duration sits beside its causes; then the trend reader can fit duration on tests, cache misses and load and
     report the unexplained residual, which is all the alert fires on.
15. **W1798** (ready) Each py_test target declares MIKEMOL_TEST_CPU_S from its measured CPU and a raised wall timeout as
   the backstop
   - next: Once mikemol.gate.target.cpu.seconds{kind=pytest} has a day of samples (query-metrics), derive per target
     budget = a multiple of its max CPU with the derivation written beside the number, declare MIKEMOL_TEST_CPU_S in the
     dist's test macro and raise timeout to a backstop; start with hooks.
16. **W1628** (ready) The edit gate judges a file that is new on disk under its real module name, so a
   [[tool.mypy.overrides]] for that module applies
17. **W1662** (ready) A REFUSED verdict prints the refusing stage's own FAIL, HOST and HELD lines, not only the gate's
   last line
18. **W1663** (ready) A REFUSED verdict gives the one command that reproduces only the refusing rung
19. **W1689** (ready) //ratchet:ruff and //ratchet:ratchet_gate reported red BEFORE the probe (CONTROL FAILED) in the
   refused 30-entry gate and are green after: reproduce alone under load and repair the cause
   - next: Reproduce //ratchet:ruff and //ratchet:ratchet_gate alone under load; read the CONTROL FAILED output to see
     whether the red-before-probe is the same victim contention as W1688, and if so close this as its consequence with
     that evidence.
20. **W1704** (ready) A stack's submit also refuses a declared mutant whose file or pattern is absent from the tip tree,
   and a type error found by mypy over the tip tree
   - next: At submit, run the real lint, mutation/.venv/bin/python3 -m mikemol.mutation.lint DIST over each dist whose
     mutants.regex a tip entry carries (the command preflight.sh line 218 and W1531 use, so the submit check and the
     preflight cannot disagree), in place of my scratch /home/mikemol/mtools-stacks/lint_mutants.py; then a
     mypy-over-the-tip refusal from each dist's venv. A missing mutation venv refuses, as preflight does.
21. **W1706** (ready) A withdrawn train leader ends only its own group and returns its joined followers to the queue,
   instead of being refused while it leads any
22. **W1716** (ready) A train leader says so on stderr as each round ends: which groups it gated, red or green, which
   prefix it landed, and what it rebuilds next
   - next: After W1715 records each round to the services: print one stderr line per round as it ends (groups gated, red
     or green, the prefix landed, what is rebuilt next), so a long train does not read as a hang; the same fields as the
     record.
23. **W1725** (ready) A submit refuses a stack entry whose frozen blob of a file differs from HEAD plus the entries
   ahead of it, unless the entry deliberately changes that file
   - next: At submit, for each entry's frozen files, compare the blob with the file as HEAD plus the entries ahead of it
     leaves it: a difference the entry did not ask for (its diff against that predecessor touches lines no card of the
     entry names) is refused naming the file and the entry, as stackimports refuses a later module. The W1603 family;
     the case it would have caught is mega's cli.py carrying location= before W1687's ops.py.
24. **W1734** (ready) Retire MIKEMOL_COMMIT_LOCK_TIMEOUT, a wall-clock bound on the commit lease wait
   - next: In mtools only commit_kata.TIMEOUT_ENV (line 126) and the gateenv KEEP list name it; no tracked caller sets
     it. Census other repos' callers (git grep across ~/github), give each an event exit (holder pid gone, or
     mikemol-commit --withdraw), then remove the knob. Operator rule 2026-09-23, relayed by github-8f 2026-10-19.
25. **W1736** (ready) Find which targets drive bazel_test's +58.8 s per gate slope, and shard them
   - next: After a week of mikemol.gate.target.wall.seconds (from 2026-10-20), rank targets by wall growth between gates
     that ran them fresh and shard the top; exclude gates whose fresh count is the whole suite.
26. **W1737** (ready) The gate step series carry round as a label so a step in round k compares like with like
   - next: Add round to the W1718/W1722 step series. Stack id is unbounded, so it goes on the span, not on a metric
     label (the lesson of W1732); luthen's registry ceiling decides. github-8f 2026-10-19.
27. **W1746** (ready) A submit refuses a stack whose file imports a module that neither HEAD nor an earlier entry of
   that stack holds
   - next: First give stackimports the frozen paths of every stack queued ahead (read each queued key's snapshot via
     stacksnap); then a module on disk, absent from HEAD, this stack up to the entry and every stack ahead is a finding
     naming it as held by no stack ahead. Test: ahead-stack holds it passes, nothing ahead holds it is refused.
28. **W1754** (ready) A stuck commit train is stopped by the user-level apply loop, not routed to the operator
   - next: Operator ruling 2026-10-20 (695f3e0a line 86839, uuid ae2dc725-c4f4-43d4-9bbf-12e1618aef7e, verified, origin
     human): stopping a train needs no sudo, no novel secret access, and not the operator; two auto-apply tf loop
     services exist, one for root and one for nonroot. This session ran requeue.py as itself and the stop worked
     (2026-10-20: group 2018173 gone, the consolidated stack became the leader). Declare the stop as a row in the
     nonroot apply set (luthen's declared apply, the user-level loop) so the loop runs it when a leader outlives a
     learned multiple of the gate median, and the next stuck train never routes to the operator. Needs luthen to host
     the row; mtools states the condition and the idempotent command.
29. **W1758** (ready) Find the hooks pytest that spun for an hour in the withdrawn W1753 gate
   - next: github-8f 2026-10-20 (the house rule: CPU time, never wall clock; RLIMIT_CPU plus core size 0, operator
     2026-09-23). (1) Run the gate's pytest under an RLIMIT_CPU learned from its own history: a multiple of the trend
     median CPU of that step (mikemol-gate-trend), the derivation written beside the number and never a typed constant,
     so a spin ends as a refusal that names itself, not a one-hour burn. (2) A pytest plugin writes one record per test
     as it starts and finishes (to the journal, per W1744's channel): the spinning test is then the one with a start and
     no finish, a query, not a mystery. (3) Still reproduce the W1753 tree in a scratch worktree to learn what spun.
     Sequence: the plugin first (it answers which test), then the limit.
30. **W1761** (ready) One site finder for mutation, imported by both mutate.py and mutate_runner.py
   - next: mutation/mutate.py's def_sites and check_mutants/mutate_runner.py's _owned/sites are two programs that find
     mutation sites, and the 2026-10-20 Protocol fix had to land twice because they drifted (the dist grid runs the
     runner's, the declared-spec scope uses def_sites). Consolidate into one finder in the mutation dist that the runner
     imports (it already imports mikemol.mutation.declarations), keeping the runner's qualified-path and #n repeat
     labels and def_sites' one-liner policy as explicit options, and run the wedge or reuse check to confirm the shared
     core. github-8f 2026-10-20.
31. **W1765** (ready) Mutants shards take their cores from a non-blocking fence/membudget ledger lease instead of
   sampling load1, so shards starting together divide the free cores (bounded by construction)
32. **W1779** (ready) The mutants budget is read from luthen's model trained on the per-target series (instructions,
   cycles, CPU, wall, children)
   - next: The emit (W1783) landed and luthen collects the series; what remains is reading the mutants budget from
     luthen's trained model once it serves one, instead of the declared cpu_budget; ask luthen-observability for the
     model's read endpoint.
33. **W317** (blocked) Plan splitting mtools into a GitHub org with one repo per distribution: what moves, what shared
   infrastructure goes where, migration order
   - blocked on: W319
   - next: Umbrella: the org mikemol-tools, each repo with its history, mtools the workstream repo, shared machinery as
     atomic composed modules. Order per .claude/design/W317-split-plan.md
34. **W1109** (blocked) Profile one commit gate under the job cap: peak running actions over time (bazel --profile) and
   the same commit's wall time idle versus loaded
   - blocked on: W1667
35. **W1116** (blocked) mikemol-gate-ledger --profile: per check CPU seconds, refusal rate, first-failure rank and a
   refused-because histogram, no wall-clock figure
   - blocked on: W1115
36. **W1122** (blocked) mtools' own pre-commit records each check to a gate ledger with mikemol-gate-ledger --record
   (live hook: tool first, hook last)
   - blocked on: W1667
37. **W1153** (blocked) Operator act: register trusted publishers for each project name on PyPI and TestPyPI, bound to
   the publish workflow and environment (no stored token)
   - blocked on: operator: act register trusted publishers: create GitHub environments testpypi and pypi on
     mikemol/mtools, then add a pending publisher for each of the 34 mikemol-`<dir>` projects on test.pypi.org and
     pypi.org (owner mikemol, repository mtools, workflow publish.yml, environment testpypi or pypi); steps verbatim in
     .claude/design/pypi-publish.md
   - next: OPERATOR RULED: 68 forms with a TestPyPI rehearsal. Steps, each one command or one generated worksheet: (A)
     PYTHONPATH=pkgbuild/src python3 -m mikemol.pkgbuild.wheel environments mikemol/mtools --write : makes the testpypi
     and pypi environments (pypi needs your approval). (B1) PYTHONPATH=pkgbuild/src python3 -m mikemol.pkgbuild.wheel
     publishers . --index testpypi --html /tmp/testpypi-publishers.html : worksheet of 34 rows, tick each after Add,
     done when it says 34 of 34 done. (C1) gh workflow run publish.yml -f index=testpypi , then install check from
     TestPyPI (W1152). (B2) the same publishers command with --index pypi and /tmp/pypi-publishers.html : 34 more rows.
     (C2) gh workflow run publish.yml -f index=pypi , then approve the pypi environment. Undo: delete a pending
     publisher on its page, it reserves nothing until the first upload. Depends on W1217 landing (A and B).

60. **W841** (blocked) bazel admits each ACTION before it materializes data into zram, letting running actions finish
   and zram's LRU GC drain between them
   - blocked on: W1667
   - next: Operator 2026-10-06 (replaces the watchdog/stop-a-running-job design): the key is an admission check prior to
     the executor copying data into zram, catching actions as they are about to begin; existing actions continue; zram's
     own GC cycle drains it LRU-style, so waiting for it to fall is sound (not a permanent pin). Measured on this bazel
     (bazel help build --long): no built-in dynamic per-action gate, but three native pieces. (1)
     --local_extra_resources=zram_mb=N plus target tag resources:zram_mb:X makes bazel's own scheduler queue an action
     until the resource frees (static N per command: size it from zram headroom at command start minus a margin; only
     tagged targets take part, so tag them in dist_checks/gate_stages). (2) --run_under (tests) and --shell_executable
     (shell actions) run a wrapper before each such action: wait there on the LIVE zram reading, with membudget's zram
     gate (W837) as the waiter. (3) --sandbox_base picks where per-action sandbox trees go. Not yet shown: a hook that
     gates every local action type dynamically (compile/py actions run through the sandbox binary, not a shell). Next:
     prototype (2) for tests and (1) for the heavy macro targets on mtools, measure whether the heavy zram writers are
     covered, and only then decide whether the remainder needs remote execution (remote_executor is already configured).
     Still open from the earlier measurement: the staged-tree wrapper path and adoption in the other bazel repos.

  before 2026-09-23 names W17.

  someone runs fence --ratchet on a subject in a pod. ⚑ Lesson (cassian's): a block must name the INSTRUMENT, never a
